中東緊張暫緩，風險資產全面反攻
2026 年 5 月
4 月全球主要股市報酬全面轉正，與 3 月跌勢形成鮮明對比，AI 題材再度成為主軸，重燃市場熱
度，風險資產動能強勁回溫。以美元計價 4 月表現觀察，新興亞洲與新興市場上漲分別上漲 17.4%
與 14.5%，表現最為亮麗，主要受台灣與南韓在 AI 半導體供應鏈帶動下的強勁買盤支撐；標普 500
的 4 月漲幅也創 2020 年 11 月以來最佳月表現 。 然而 ， 拉丁美洲與東協市因與科技題材連結度較低，
使其期間漲幅相對緩和。不過，MSCI 拉丁美洲指數今年前 4 個月的漲幅仍高達 17.3%，為所有主要

股市之冠。
隨著市場風險接受度提高，全球主要債市4 月也同步反彈。新興當地債券單月上漲2.79%表現最
佳，主要因美元指數當月由約 100 降至近 98，使新興當地債換算回美元後的價格水漲船高。波動較
大的亞洲高收益債、新興美元債券、全球高收益債表現也有所表現。全球投資級公司債、全球主權債
券等債券雖亦分別上漲 1.36%、1.25%，但幅度上相對落後。
4 月市場大幅反彈的核心驅動力來自三方面：其一，投資人對中東戰事可能導致通膨上揚的疑慮
明顯鈍化；其二，AI 主軸再度成為主導行情的關鍵主題，包括 Alphabet 雲端與 AI 業務需求暢旺帶
動半導體類股大漲；其三，總經基本面韌性意外優於預期，整體勞動市場仍穩定。然而，霍爾木茲海
峽運輸尚未完全恢復，中東戰事變數仍存，地緣風險仍是市場最大不確定性來源。
根據彭博 5/4 的統計，今年 Q1 與 Q2 EPS 年增率均可望突破兩成，我們預期 2026 年標普 500
企業獲利增長動能持續 ，AI 仍是核心驅動主軸。儘管短期股市可能因中東戰事帶來通膨隱憂而波動，

但我們認為企業基本面強度足以抵銷中東衝突與政策不確定性 ，而企業強勁的獲利成長將可望為全球
股市中期走勢帶來支撐。
債市方面，受到美伊戰爭影響，4 月的 FOMC 會議會後聲明偏鷹，壓抑市場降息預期，考量後續
美伊和談時程仍有不確定性 ，預期長天期公債殖利率波動風險將加劇 。 由於目前美國中短天期公債殖
利率顯著高於過去 10 年均值，且中短期債券到期期間較短，具備資金靈活運用彈性，因此建議在債
券天期選擇上 ， 以中短天期美國公債及高評級投資級債為主 ， 並以平衡投資組合波動角度做適當配置 。

美國股市：美股盈餘增長強勁，AI 主題重獲市場青睞
4 月美股雖受伊朗戰爭不確定性干擾，在財報優異及 AI 產業高速成長帶動下，標普 500 指數續
創歷史新高。指數上揚主要由輝達、Alphabet、亞馬遜、博通、蘋果、微軟、超微、英特爾、美光
等科技股帶動，表現相對弱勢的族群包括埃克森美孚、雪佛龍、嬌生、默克藥廠、亞培、ServiceNow、
AT&T、洛克希德馬丁、雷神等，市場焦點已經從能源、國防回歸 AI 主題。
美國通膨墊高，但經濟表現仍具韌性，支持聯準會更長時間維持利率。4 月 ISM 製造業 PMI 持
平於 52.7，連續第四個月維持擴張區間。其中供應商交貨指數從 58.9 升至 60.6，反映伊朗戰爭與航

運通道風險升高，導致供應鏈壓力再度升溫；客戶庫存指數降至 39.1，持續偏低；新訂單指數小幅
上升至 54.1，訂單動能尚具支撐，積壓訂單由 54.4 大幅降至 51.4，新出口訂單進一步下滑至 47.9，
顯示外需偏弱；價格指數從 78.3 攀升至 84.6；就業指數降至 46.4，顯示企業尚未恢復積極擴編意願。
故製造業雖維持擴張 ， 但由於庫存已經偏低 ，若戰事持續恐將加速推升通膨。4 月份 ISM 服務業 PMI
指數由前月的 54.0 下降至 53.6，低於市場預期的 53.7，新訂單指數由前月的 60.6 下降至 53.5，低
於市場預期的 57.3，支付價格指數持平於 70.7，物價雖仍在高檔但並未惡化。同時預期新任聯準會
主席 Warsh 上台後，要等到現任主席鮑威爾完全辭去 Fed 理事職務，才會有較大的空間與明確度去
推進「相信 AI 將帶來生產力提振、進而支持降息」的政策論述，故降息時間或許延後，未來利率將
更長時間維持不變。
AI 帶動科技股財報表現優異。預估第一季標普 500 企業盈餘年增可達到 23.9%，且第二季盈餘

增長仍維持近兩成水準，科技板塊受惠於 AI，仍是帶動美股企業獲利增長主軸，但到了第二季，能
源價格維持高檔下將推升能源產業獲利，且若伊朗戰事無法落幕，預料各種原物料價格都將受影響走
高，標普 500 企業獲利成長性並不看淡。
美國金融股第一季財報普遍優於預期，對消費惡化與私募基金流動性的擔憂已經淡化。由於股票
與商品市場交投熱絡，帶動承銷與交易收入顯著成長，各大銀行放款規模持續擴張，淨利息收入成長
優於預期。消費信用方面，信用卡支出與消費貸款餘額仍具韌性，整體資產品質良好。
目前標普 500 在指數續創新高下本益比來到 26 倍、股價淨值比 5.36 倍，短期評價偏高，且通
膨風險逐漸增加、伊朗戰爭的不確定性仍在，雖美國科技龍頭在 AI 產業具備領先優勢，但短期需留
意市場過熱及估值偏高風險，建議以長線角度分批布局。

歐洲股市：歐洲央行態度謹慎，川普加徵歐洲汽車關稅
4 月歐股受能源價格大漲衝擊，雖財報優異但科技股比重相對美股較低，泛歐 600 指數緩步墊高。
指數上揚主要由艾司摩爾、西門子、西門子能源、匯豐、艾波比、英飛凌、施耐德電機、諾基亞、瑞
銀集團、諾和諾德等企業帶動，表現相對弱勢的族群包括殼牌、德意志電信、阿斯特捷利康、諾華、
葛蘭素史克、聯合利華、依視路羅薩奧蒂卡、赫力昂、挪威國家石油、BP 等，市場焦點已經從能源、
國防題材回歸科技與金融股。
4 月歐元區 服務業受到物價飆漲與能源支出暴增的雙重打擊， 服務 業採購經理人指數 急挫至

47.4，創疫情後最差表現；然而製造業採購經理人指數則在新訂單改善(尤其法國)及避險備貨需求的
支撐下，逆勢攀升至 52.2，然而就業人數持續萎縮，反映企業在高通膨與需求不確定性下普遍採取
縮編策略，經濟放緩格局明顯。法國製造業 PMI 攀升至 52.8，主要得益於新訂單走高，反映需求復
甦；德國製造業 PMI 雖達 51.4，增長動能依賴企業拉升庫存，能否延續仍須觀察。
儘管歐洲央行在 4 月利率決策會議按兵不動，但此舉是反映需要更多時間凝聚升息共識，並蒐集
能源衝擊對通膨的實際影響數據 。歐洲央行總裁透露委員會內部曾認真討論升息的可能性，維持不動
的理由包括：近期數據仍接近3 月預測、貨幣政策在能源價格上漲前已處於適當位置、金融與信貸條
件已開始收緊，目前局勢「偏離」原先基準情境，通膨上行風險與經濟成長下行風險均在加劇，但歐
洲經濟尚未進入停滯。市場預期 ECB 將於 2026 年升息 2 碼，甚至不排除於年底或於明年升息第 3
碼，但考量歐洲通膨仍與伊朗戰事息息相關，若能源價格回落、需求走弱，則升息時點可能延後；若

能源價格繼續在高檔盤旋、通膨持續性跡象提早浮現，不排除6 月即會啟動第一次升息。英格蘭銀行
(英國央行)在最新利率決策會議中維持利率不變，但整體立場偏向謹慎，市場同樣預期未來半年可能
有 2 碼升息幅度。近期 ECB 與 BOE 的升息預期已帶動歐元、英鎊轉強。
川普於 5/1 宣布，由於歐盟不遵守貿易協議，將對歐盟進口汽車與卡車加徵 25%關稅(不包含英
國)。由於歐洲議會在 3/26 已有條件通過歐美貿易協議的相關立法，故川普調整汽車業關稅從 15%
到 25%，可能引發美歐貿易戰再次啟動，接下來預料歐盟祭出關稅反制措施且展開新一輪談判。
泛歐 600 指數目前本益比為 16.87，股價淨值比為 2.39，目前面臨能源價格仍在高檔、川普對歐
盟汽車業加徵關稅，短期風險增加。然而考量評價合理、伊朗戰爭持續降溫且預料美歐經過談判，終
將能達成貿易協議，歐股仍可以長線角度、作為分批佈局標的。

日本股市：日本利率按兵不動，AI 題材帶動指數創高
4 月上旬日股受地緣政治風險衝擊，所幸在 AI 題材帶動下科技股走高，日經指數更於 4 月一度
收盤站上 60,537 點，以收盤價站上 6 萬點歷史新高。市場主要由軟銀、愛德萬測試、Keyence、村
田製作所、三菱電機、三菱 UFJ 金融、東京威力科創、藤倉、鎧俠、三井住友金融等公司帶動，表
現相對弱勢的族群包括三菱商事、豐田汽車、任天堂、三井物產、永旺、武田藥品、國際石油開發帝
石、7&I、山之內製藥、東方樂園等，市場焦點回歸 AI、國防，以及升息可能帶動的金融股。

相較於歐洲汽車出口至美國關稅再度被川普調高，目前並未傳出美國可能調高日本進口汽車關稅
的訊息，顯示高市早苗先前與川普會晤帶來實質外交成效。日本進入黃金週，但高市首相選擇飛往越
南與澳洲「拼外交」，尤其將澳洲定位為「準同盟」 ，推動以「最上級」護衛艦為基礎的共同開發與輸
出，顯然高市對於國防自主有堅定決心。
日本央行 4 月利率決策會議以 6 比 3 同意將利率維持於 0.75%，三位委員認為應將政策利率調
高至 1.0%，市場對後續升息的預期明顯升溫。日本央行同時將 FY2026 核心 CPI 預測大幅上調 0.9
個百分點至+2.8%，FY2027 上調 0.3 個百分點至+2.3%，這些預測建立在油價將從每桶約 105 美元
回落至 70～80 美元的假設之上 ， 故若油價長期維持高位 ， 通膨前景可能在7 月展望報告中再度上修 ，
而對 GDP 增長預期也小幅下修。對比會後聲明透露出來的鷹派立場，總裁植田和男會後記者會發言
立場則略顯保守，持續淡化央行升息時點，強調需要時間觀察經濟及通膨發展，且提及達成央行經濟

預估的前景下降 ， 尚無法確定需要多久來評估下次升息時點 。 鑒於過於弱勢的日圓匯率可能更進一步
推高國內通膨，日本央行仍有高機率於第三季上調政策利率，若油價再度上行，甚至不排除在 6 月就
採取行動。
截至 4 月 24 日當周，外資買超日股 8,079 億日圓，2026 年以來外資對日股的累計買超金額來
到 7.97 兆日圓，改寫今年以來累計加碼金額紀錄。近期日財長與外匯最高官員相繼釋出干預匯市警
告、加上外資持續匯入且市場對 BOJ 升息的預期，日圓貶值趨勢已扭轉。近期日圓一度單日升值 3%，
顯示 160 為日本政府當局匯價的干預底線，預料近期日圓仍將於 155 至 158 附近震盪。
2026 年以來日股 TOPIX 指數受惠於 AI 題材、高市擴大財政刺激、強化軍事安全，但本益比仍
在 19.39 倍，股價淨值比在 1.77 倍。近期日圓守住 160 關卡，匯率穩定可降低投資者不確定性，預
料日本企業股票回購仍持續推進，可望為日股在全球金融市場震盪下帶來支撐。

台灣股市：強勁經濟與產業基本面支撐下的長多格局
4 月台灣股市在 AI 超級週期與全球資金的簇擁下 ， 正式跨越了四萬點 ， 本次台股的突破並非單純
的資金行情，而是具備了極為堅實的總體經濟、強健的產業鏈獲利基本面，以及法規鬆綁帶來的籌碼
結構重塑。
台灣今年初展現了令全球市場矚目的經濟爆發力。主計總處公布的第一季 GDP 成長率概估高達
13.69%，創下近 39 年來的單季最高紀錄。這股強大動能的源頭，清晰地反映在經濟部的三月份外銷
訂單數據上， 三月單月接單金額飆升至 911.2 億美元 ， 年增率高達65.9%， 連續14 個月維持正成長。

資訊通信與電子產品年增率分別高達 120.9%與 73.7%，這顯示台灣在全球 AI 基礎設施、高效能運
算（HPC）以及雲端資料中心建設中，扮演著不可替代的樞紐角色。亮眼的總經數據，為台股的高檔
估值提供了最強而有力的下檔支撐。
中經院 4 月製造業 PMI 大幅跳升 4.9 個百分點至 60.3%，連續 7 個月呈現擴張，更是近 55 個月
以來最快的擴張速度。除了 AI 與半導體的實質需求外，中東地緣政治的緊張局勢促使製造業啟動了
「防禦性提前拉貨」 。短期內這將維持製造業的熱絡，但也必須持續監控輸入性通膨的潛在風險。
宏觀數據之外，台積電於 4 月中旬召開的法說會，無疑是穩住外資與台股軍心的定海神針。財報
顯示，第一季 EPS 達 22.08 元，毛利率以 66.2%飛越財測高標。其中高效能運算（HPC）相關營收
佔比已正式突破六成 （達 61%） ，並上修了2026 全年美元營收成長目標至 30% 以上。這場法說會
不僅確認了台積電高階製程的護城河，也確立了全球 AI 伺服器供應鏈的長線能見度。

在基本面一片大好之際，金管會放寬了主動型基金與主動式 ETF 對單一個股 10%的持股上限，
從 10%放寬至 25%。這項法規鬆綁產生了極為強大的「流動性溢價」效應。過去受限於10%緊箍咒
的機構、 基金資金 ， 如今得以大舉回補台積電 、 聯發科等核心權值股 。這讓大盤指數在衝破四萬點後，
資金高度向權值龍頭集中，成為加權指數的進一步推收力量。
綜合亮眼的總經數據、良好的企業基本面，以及籌碼向大型股集中的法規紅利，我們對台股整體
的長線投資建議維持加碼。然而，目前加權指數與季線的正乖離率已來到 18%的歷史相對高位，意
味著市場隨時可能出現技術性拉回與獲利了結的賣壓。必須強調的是，牛市中的急跌與拉回屬於 「健
康的籌碼換手」 ，並非長線趨勢的反轉。面對當前乖離頗大、但長線看多的盤勢，建議投資人不追高，
待拉回後布局，預留部分現金，保留流動性以迎接拉回時的絕佳買點。

科技類股：半導體產業持續看好，分批佈局雲端巨頭
回顧 4 月份，在地緣政治影響逐漸淡化之後，華爾街對 AI 硬體的「錯失恐懼症（FOMO） 」全面
爆發。費城半導體指數上演了史無前例的連續 18 個交易日上漲，單月狂飆近 40%。這波資金浪潮不
僅將指數推向歷史新高。支撐這波半導體狂飆的是極其扎實的財報利多，不僅英特爾靠著晶圓代工與
CPU 獲利超預期迎來大復活，記憶體族群更步入了史無前例的「超級循環」 。由於AI 伺服器對 HBM
與高階 SSD 需求爆發，導致記憶體陷入結構性大缺貨。美光（Micron）與威騰（WDC）等大廠握

有絕對的定價權，這再次確認了半導體作為「AI 算力基礎設施」的長線多頭地位。
在剛公布的財報中，四大雲端巨頭交出了令人驚豔的成績單。微軟的 AI 相關年度經常性收入大
增，Google Cloud 也寫下逾 60%的驚人成長，Meta 則透過 AI 優化廣告投放，拉升利潤率，亞馬
遜（AWS）的營收增速也創下近期新高。這些數據證明，掌握龐大生態系的巨頭，已經能將 AI 算力
成功轉化為自身業務的現金流。此外，根據 2026 Q1 財報數據，四大雲端服務供應商並未因高基期
而放慢腳步，反而持續加大投入，全年資本支出（Capex）預計由原先估計的 6,000 億美元進一步增
加至近 7,000 億美元。在這波驚人的支出中除了晶片設計商外，代工的台積電、供應HBM 的三星與
海力士，以及伺服器組裝與散熱解決方案的廠商都因此受惠，這也是硬體續漲的主要原因。
然而儘管 CSP 業者的支出續增，但投資人顯然不再全面買單，市場進入「AI 變現檢驗期」 。由於
龐大的折舊壓力與變現的陣痛 ，四大 CSP 業者在財報後的股價表現各不相同 。Google 與亞馬遜成功

用現有核心業務的強勁現金流護航 AI 支出，成為資金的避風港；反觀微軟與 Meta，因資本支出預
期大幅暴增 ， 市場擔憂長期支出深不見底 ，變現遙遙無期 ，積極的佈局反而成為短期壓抑股價的包袱 。
然而四大 CSP 的 EPS 年增率依然維持在雙位數，但評價面卻因資本開支焦慮被壓低，下檔風險相對
受控，這提供了一個逢低布局的機會。
AI 產業的長多格局不變，但在進入 5 月後，市場可能面臨傳統的「Sell in M ay」季節性逆風，
且 2026 年適逢美國期中選舉，歷史經驗顯示第二、三季往往是波動最劇烈的時期。因此，我們建議
半導體類股仍是不可或缺的持股，但也應該開始逢低布局美股七巨頭等大型科技類股。 七巨頭的預估
本益比已經回落至合理區間，且具備穩健的現金流。加上隨著 Google 的 TPU、亞馬遜的 Trainium
等自研晶片部署比例提升，CSP 業者將逐步降低對高價 GPU 的依賴，這將顯著降低長期推論成本。
因此建議適時布局相關類股，來迎接下半年變現能力轉強後的估值修復行情。

AI 電力類股：資料中心用電需求倍增，有利電力基礎建設發展
IEA(國際能源署)發布 2026 年 Key Questions on Energy and AI 報告，指出受到 AI 快速發展影
響，全球用電需求將急遽成長，有利於相關電力基礎建設發展。IEA 指出，全球資料中心用電量在 2024
至 2025 年間成長逾 15%，年增約 70 太瓦時至 485 太瓦時，占全球電力需求略超過 1.5%，未來幾
年用電需求更將進一步加速，IEA 預估全球資料中心用電將從 2024 年的 415 太瓦時倍增至 2030 年
的 950 太瓦時，屆時將占全球電力需求的 3%，AI 專用資料中心用電量更將成長三倍至 465 太瓦時。

驅動電力需求暴增的核心因素是 AI 伺服器功耗密度的指數級攀升。以 Nvidia 為例，2020 年
Ampere 架構機架功耗密度約 13 千瓦時，當前 Blackwell 架構已達 130 千瓦時，下一代 Rubin 架構
將躍升至 600 千瓦時， 屆時單一機架尖峰用電量將等同約 65 戶家庭 。在 AI 伺服器功耗上升的同時，
大型科技巨頭資本支出也持續攀升，為 AI 伺服器帶來更多需求，2025 年科技巨頭資本支出合計已超
過 4,000 億美元，2026 年預計再增 75%至 7,150 億美元，IEA 更進一步預估 2026 至 2030 年間資
料中心累計投資將達 3.9 兆美元，隨資料中心大量建置，AI 用電需求將隨之攀升。
此外，IEA 指出儘管單一 AI 任務的能耗效率正以前所未見的速度改善，單一任務功耗每年約可
降至十分之一，然而新興高耗能應用如影片生成、推理及代理式 AI 也持續推陳出新，這些新功能的
能耗可達到簡單文字生成的數百至數千倍 ，導致即便 AI 效率持續提升，整體能耗需求仍然有增無減。

在 AI 用電需求大增的同時，目前電力基礎建設供給端各環節均出現嚴重瓶頸，相關關鍵零組件
因產能不足，導致交期持續延長，包含變壓器交期平均需二至三年，燃氣輪機交期長達五年，電網併
聯在美國多數地區也須排隊長達五至十年。以德州 ERCOT 獨立電網為例，資料中心併網排隊量從
2024 年 12 月的 63 吉瓦時暴增至 2026 年 1 月逾 230 吉瓦時，但該州歷史尖峰負載僅 85 吉瓦時，
顯示電力需求遠超既有電網承載量。可預見隨電力需求持續攀升，相關電力基礎建設上中下游未來訂
單積壓量都可望保持高水位，並隨公司產能陸續開出，逐步轉換為公司營收貢獻。
展望後市，IEA 預估全球資料中心發電量至 2030 年將倍增至超過 1,000 太瓦時，其中再生能源
與天然氣各占約三分之一，分別達 360 及 340 太瓦時，核能預料將從 75 太瓦時成長至 120 太瓦時，
故各類相關零組件包含燃氣輪機、變壓器、核能及儲能等子產業都可望受惠這一 AI 用電趨勢，隨 AI
電力需求持續上升，對於各類電力上中下游供應鏈都可正向看待。

能源類股：海峽受阻導致油價居高不下；阿聯酋退出 OPEC 有利戰後供給恢復
西德州原油期貨價格 4 月再度攀升至每桶 100 美元之上，主因經荷莫茲海峽的石油出口持續受
阻，加上美伊談判持續未見明顯進展，同時期間內美國總統川普已數次拒絕伊朗所提出之協議，美國
對伊朗的封鎖行動也持續進行，故在全球石油供需缺口維持的狀況下，全球石油庫存水位不斷下降，
為石油價格帶來上行壓力。
國際能源署 4 月月報指出，受荷莫茲海峽供應中斷影響，3 月全球石油供應暴跌 1,010 萬桶/日
至每日 9,700 萬桶，其中 OPEC+產量月減 940 萬桶/日至每日 4,240 萬桶，非 OPEC+供應月減 77

萬桶/日至每日 5,470 萬桶，儘管沙烏地西岸、阿聯酋富查伊拉港及伊拉克經土耳其傑伊漢的 ITP 管
線替代路線出口量，已從戰前每日不到 400 萬桶提升至每日 720 萬桶，然而仍無法彌補荷莫茲海峽
的供給中斷，使得中東海灣國家石油出口損失合計仍高達每日 1,300 萬桶，自開戰以來，3 月石油供
給損失已超過 3.6 億桶，國際能源署更進一步預估 4 月石油供給損失將擴大至 4.4 億桶。
在供給大幅下降的同時，國際能源署也同步下修全球石油需求預估，預計 2026 年全年需求將年
減 8 萬桶/日，較上月報告下修 73 萬桶/日，其中 3 月全球需求預計將年減 80 萬桶/日，4 月進一步
擴大至年減 230 萬桶/日，合計第二季需求預估年減 150 萬桶/日，為新冠疫情以來最大單季跌幅，
下調需求預估主因荷莫茲海峽供給中斷，導致眾多仰賴中東石油進口的亞洲塑化廠面臨斷供，在缺乏
原料的狀況下被迫降低需求。
然而儘管目前荷莫茲海峽持續受阻，導致油價居高不下，然而戰後有利油價快速下行的因子也持

續在萌芽。阿聯酋 4/28 宣布退出 OPEC 及 OPEC+，為該組織近年來最重要的成員流失。阿聯酋目
前石油產能為 485 萬桶/日 ， 然而其受限於OPEC 配額 ， 實際產量僅約360 萬桶/日 ， 兩者差距達125
萬桶/日，過去幾年來阿聯酋持續因產量受限問題，而與其他成員國出現爭執，隨阿聯酋退出 OPEC，
將有利於其提升產量上限。雖然目前因荷莫茲海峽仍處封鎖狀態，阿聯酋石油出口同樣受阻，然而一
旦戰後海峽重開，阿聯酋將可不再受 OPEC 配額約束，立即釋放約 125 桶/日的增量供給，阿聯酋國
有石油公司 ADNOC 更規劃將在 2027 年將石油產量上升至 500 萬桶/日、2029 年再進一步上升至
600 萬桶/日。此外阿聯酋退出 OPEC 亦可能引發其他成員跟進 ，使得戰後油價下行風險將顯著增加。
展望後市，當前油價雖受到海峽封鎖影響，中短線上有望持續維持在 100 美元左右震盪，然而
美伊戰事未見進一步擴大，後續油價也難以再創新高，此外若未來美伊談判出現進展，海峽運量出現

緩解，阿聯酋增產也有利戰後原油供需恢復，故我們目前對於能源類股後市中性看待。

黃金類股：全球央行續買黃金，惟短線金價仍受美伊戰爭不利影響
黃金價格 4 月持續在區間內震盪，黃金類股表現也相對弱勢，主因受到美伊戰爭影響，油價上漲
帶動通膨預期攀升，使得市場對於聯準會降息預期下滑，帶動配置黃金的機會成本上升，同時避險
情緒導致美元走強，也不利於黃金等以美元計價的商品資產表現。此外，受到戰爭影響，土耳其動
用黃金儲備以支應外匯與流動性需求，短線拋售黃金也導致金價承壓。
世界黃金協會數據顯示，2026 年 3 月各國央行申報合計淨賣出 30 公噸黃金，主要就是受到土
耳其拋售 60 公噸所拖累，受此影響，合計第一季全球央行申報黃金淨購買量僅有 2 頓。然而若看加

計未申報買盤量，則是完全不同的狀況，根據世界黃金協會調查顯示，2026 年第一季全球央行黃金
購買量加計未通報買盤後，合計淨購買 244 噸，為自 2024 年第四季以來的新高，同時顯著高於過
去五年平均購買水準的 228 噸，顯示檯面下的世界央行購金趨勢仍未改變。
另一方面，在高盛 4 月 29 日發布的最新報告中，維持了對於 2026 年底黃金目標價 5,400 美
元/盎司的預測。高盛指出，目前金價主要驅動因素包括全球央行持續推進外匯儲備多元化、目前相
對偏低的投機性部位回歸常態化，以及聯準會預計在年內降息 50 個基點等三大支撐。在央行需求方
面，高盛指出，在其央行會議調查中，約 70%的受訪央行預期全球黃金儲備將繼續上升。在風險評
估方面，高盛認為短線風險偏向下行，主要因荷莫茲海峽封鎖若持續 ，可能觸發債市或股市修正，
進而引發黃金部位遭到清算， 然而中長期風險則偏向上行，因 伊朗事件與更廣泛的地緣政治發展 ，
如格陵蘭及委內瑞拉議題，可能加速資金轉向黃金配置。
另一方面，來自穩定幣發行商 Tether 的黃金買盤也值得關注。截至 2026 年 3 月底，Tether 為

支撐 USDT 儲備而持有的黃金市值上升至 198 億美元，約為 132 公噸，較 2025 年 12 月底的 126
公噸再增加約 6 公噸 ，此外 Tether 另持有 22 公噸黃金以支撐其黃金代幣 XAUT， 兩項產品合計持倉
達約 154 公噸 ， 此規模已足以排進全球前20 名黃金持有者 。JPMorgan 貴金屬研究主管 Greg Shearer
指出，Tether 的購金行為已對金價產生實質性影響，其 2025 年全年購買量僅次於波蘭央行，並超越
所有其他央行，顯示加密貨幣機構儲備已成為黃金新一個重要買盤來源
展望後市，中長線而言央行增持黃金的結構性趨勢未變，美伊衝突加上格陵蘭、委內瑞拉等地緣
政治風險，也都有望加速各國對黃金的分散配置需求 ，使得黃金有望獲得更多來自央行買盤的支撐。
然而短線上，在美伊戰爭尚未底定前，黃金機會成本上升及部分央行拋售壓力仍將壓抑短線走勢，
故預估黃金將在區間內震盪，目前對於黃金類股後市抱持中性看法。

### Page 10

任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決策，應審慎衡量本
身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元大銀行所有，非經本行同意，
不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕不另行通知。
固定收益：華許入主 Fed 機率高，美債波動料加大
FOMC 在 4/29 的會議再度將聯邦基金利率目標區間維持在 3.50-3.75%。然而，理事 Miran 主
張降息一碼，而會後聲明中「傾於寬鬆」(easing bias)的措辭也遭三位地區主席(Cleveland 的
Hammack、Minneapolis 的 Kashkari 與 Dallas 的 Logan )反對 ，會議表決出現 4 票異議 ，創 FOMC
逾 30 年來委員立場最分歧的局面。前述三位地區聯儲主席反對寬鬆的措辭，導致市場對 Fed 後續降
息預期顯著弱化。

此外，川普提名的下任 Fed 主席 Kevin Warsh(華許)於 4/29 參議院銀行委員會審查通過，預計
於 5/11 當週進行全院表決。目前參議院共和黨、民主黨的議員比例為 53：47(含兩名傾向民主黨的
無黨籍議員)，由於除了 Tillis 以外的共和黨參議員均未公開就華許的資格表達反對，未來在參院全院
的表決中，華許也可望取得過半票數通過該任命案。若時程安排得宜，料可於 5/15 鮑爾主席任滿後
接掌 Fed。值得留意的是，鮑爾在 4 月的記者會表示將繼續留任理事會，這將導致未來的 FOMC 將
出現新舊主席並存的罕見現象。
綜觀華許的主要貨幣政策主張為「降息」 +「縮表」 ，兩者在貨幣政策方向呈現相反的效果，且
對不同天期的債券影響亦有差異： 「降息」對於雖使債券殖利率下修，但對短端影響較大；而「縮表」
量化緊縮(QT)雖屬貨幣緊縮政策，但堪薩斯聯儲研究(Smith & Valcarcel 2021)顯示，「縮表」推升期
限溢酬的幅度大於短期利率。兩者同時施行可能造成導致未來美債殖利率曲線淨陡峭化。話雖如此，

美國貨幣政策需要 FOMC 票委以多數決通過，前主席 Yellen 認為華許即便就任 Fed 主席，在表決中
仍難以取得足以支持其貨幣政策路線的票數。因此，即便 Warsh 上任，其政策主張(尤其在縮表的議
題上)短期將無法付緒實現，故預期在可見的未來對美國的貨幣政策走向改變有限。
然而，Fed 主席的權力在於議程設定與對外溝通，華許在聽證會上所表達的部分想法-包括降低
FOMC 會議與記者會舉辦的頻率等，將使 FOMC 決策透明度降低，或導致美國債市波動加大。其表
達改變目前聯準會青睞的通膨指標-核心 PCE 的意圖，也可能使金融市場的不確定性增加。
預期短線債市在通膨疑慮與政策不確定性的交錯下 ， 將持續面臨殖利率高檔震盪的壓力， 但考量
美伊全面開戰可能性極低，油價的通膨疑慮料隨時間鈍化，而 Warsh 立場傾向支持降息，因此就中
期來看，美債殖利率應有走降空間。同時，我們認為中短天期債相較於長天期債券殖利率相去不遠，
投資人無需承擔較高的存續期間更可享有不錯的債息收益 ， 且中短天期到期期間較短亦有資金靈活運

用的優勢，因此建議可以中短天期美國公債/投資級債為主。

### Page 1

Nien Made Enterprise

3Q preview: EPS +13% YoY to NT$6.6; 
Better 2H but not yet a turnaround

Reiterate Rating: BUY | PO: 441.00 TWD | Price: 358.50 TWD

3Qe EPS NT$6.6: strong rev and GP offset by opex and FX 
Nien Made is scheduled to report 3Q26 results on 4 Nov. Disclosed monthly sales imply 
3Q26 revenue growth of 16% YoY to NT$8.5bn, or 9% in USD terms, largely 
volume-driven. Growth reaccelerated from 1.7% YoY in 1H26, led by custom-made on a 
low US base and expansion in Europe and Japan. Ready-made also improved as 
Cambodia labor shortages eased. (See Exhibit 1 for US window coverings spending 
trends). Gross margin: We expect underlying GPM to rise 1.1ppt YoY to 59.8% on lower 
tariffs, broadly flat QoQ. We forecasts an NT$300mn of IEEPA tariff refunds in 2H26. 
Assuming NT$150mn is booked in 3Q, this would add 1.7ppt and lift reported GPM to 
61.5%. We expect the selling expense ratio to fall 1.7ppt QoQ to 17.5% on lower fuel 
charges, but remain 0.5ppt higher YoY due to continued salesforce and distribution 
network investment. We expect an FX loss in 3Q26 vs. an NT$118mn gain in 3Q25, 
weighing on YoY earnings growth. We forecast 3Q26 EPS of NT$6.6, up 13% YoY. 
US window covering picking up, but no turnaround yet  
BofA credit/debit card data suggests US spending on window coverings turned positive 
in Aug-26 for the first time after 19 consecutive months of YoY declines. Spending rose 
4%/3% YoY in Aug/Sep, respectively. This improvement partly explains the recent 
reacceleration in Nien Made's monthly sales. That said, we view the improvement more 
as normalization off a low base than the start of an industry turnaround. The Aug-Sep 
2025 comparison base was relatively weak, while the current rate-hike environment 
remains a headwind to housing-related demand. 
Slightly raise FY26 EPS est. to NT$22.9; PO to NT$441 
We raise our FY26 EPS estimate by 2% to NT$22.9 to reflect the blended impact of 
stronger-than-expected revenue growth and FX loss. We maintain our FY26 DPS forecast 
of NT$17 per share. We raise our PO by 4% to NT$441, as we roll forward our valuation 
basis from FY26E EPS to FY27E EPS. At the same time, we lower our target P/E from 
19x to 17x, reflecting the MSCI removal and a less supportive rate environment.

Key Metrics      
* For full definitions of iQmethod SM measures, see page 7.

Company Sector 
Interior Furnishings

Company Description 
Nien Made, founded in 1974, is one of the world's largest 
window-covering manufacturers. It has roughly 50% 
market share in both ready-made (OEM business) shades 
and blinds, and custom-made (own brand) shutters in the 
US. The US market accounted for over 75% of sales. Key 
clients include Home Depot and Wal-Mart for ready-made 
shades and blinds. Nien Made operates its own retail 
network and e-commerce business for its custom-made 
shutters under the Norman brand name in the US and 
Europe.

Investment Rationale 
We have Buy rating on Nien Made, mainly given 1) clear 
custom-made growth profile (share gain in the US and fast-
growing DTC in Japan and Europe), 2) its largest customer 
Home Depot is gaining market share, and 3) margin 
expansion potentials backed by favorable products mix.

### Page 4

Price objective basis & risk 
Nien Made Enterprise (XNRBF) 
Our PO NT$441 is derived from 17x P/E 2027E EPS. This is around 0.5SD above 
historical the one-year forward P/E, which we think is warranted given the visibility of 
the growth profile.

Downside risks are 1) weaker-than-expected Home Depot's sell-through, 2) slow 
recovery of US housing turnover, and 3) higher tariff increases on China exports to the 
US. 4) Loss of key customers.

iQmethod are: A consistently structured, detailed, and transparent methodology. Guidelines to maximize the effectiveness of the comparative valuation process, and to identify some common pitfalls. 
iQdatabase ®  is our real-time global research database that is sourced directly from our equity analysts’ earnings models and includes forecasted as well as historical data for income statements, balance sheets, and cash 
flow statements for companies covered by BofA Global Research.

leverage. We lift our earnings projection for 2026/2027 /28 by 9%/ 25%/9%. We
maintain our Buy with TP$6,800 (30x of 2027 /28 EPS, from 35x previously, mainly
on recent macro concerns and concerns on AI capex beyond 2028).

### Page 7

Mediatek (2454.TW)
21 October 2026 Citi Research
7
Mediatek
Company description
Mediatek (MTK) is a fabless IC company that designs SoC for smartphone,
smart TV, and digital media devices. It also develops chips for connectivity,
networking, smart home gadgets, and automotive sectors. It was founded in
1997, is listed on the Taiwan Stock Exchange, and is headquartered in Taiwan.
It has sales and research subsidiaries in mainland China, Singapore, India,

the US, Japan, Korea, Denmark, and England. It primarily sells its products in
China and other emerging-market countries.
Investment strategy
We rate Mediatek shares as Buy. We expect Mediatek to ride on 5G migration
in emerging markets and market share gain of its high-end Dimensity series in
China market as well. In particular, we see strong growth potential from its AI
ASIC, NTN-satellite business in the long term. We believe solid fundamentals

and strong operating leverage will support Mediatek’s margins outlook.
Valuation
Our NT$6,800 target price (rounded) for Mediatek is based on 30x P/E to
2027 /2028 EPS, which is 1-std above its five-year average forward P/E given
the promising outlook from its AI ASIC business starting from 2H26. We
believe our target PE is also justified by Mediatek's global and regional peers'
trading at an average of c32x PER. AI ASIC development with CSPs should

provide more upside from 2027 onwards. We also see Mediatek as well
prepared for 6G and 6G+NTN Satellite communication. Despite lukewarm
smartphone overall deamand, we expect Mediatek to benefit from 5G
migration in emerging markets and its Dimensity series should also gain
shares in flagship models. Non-smartphone business is also seeing
stabilizing TV and Io T demand with Mediatek outgrowing the overall market
on its better technology in WiFi 7 and connectivity.

Risks
Key downside risks that could prevent the shares from reaching our target
price include: 1) weaker-than-expected growth from AI ASIC business; 2)
more severe competition in the smartphone segment; 3) slower overall
demand related to macroeconomic risks, especially for smartphone demand;
and 4) further price hikes from foundries.
Prepared for Kevin Lu

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.
Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst

expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment
rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient

valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating
and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of

information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price
chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and

investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the
expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.

Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:
Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in

reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is
moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

information. The information contained in this report may differ from information a private company may subsequently make

within reports is at Citi's discretion, in accordance with internal policies.

OSAT 展望樂觀，SOIC 機台具技術優勢 
 2027 主要成長動能仍為 OSAT:2026 年弘塑產能擴充一倍 ，1Q26 已
經完成擴建 ，3Q26 新產能已經滿載。 外包部分 ， 目前外包比例33%，
未來佔比會下降，目前外包月產能一個月 5-6 台，因為外包產能受
限，同業也在搶外包產能。2027 主要成長動能仍為 OSAT，今年晶
圓代工客戶營收高於 OSAT，2027 年 OSAT 營收超過晶圓代工客戶，
SOIC 機台去年小量，今年量增，2027 跟 2026 差不多略增，2028
才會明顯大增，主要還是跟隨晶圓代工客戶 SOIC 擴廠進度。 
 2028-2029 年 PLP 機台出貨量會很明顯放大:PLP 及 CoPoS 設備機
台單價很高，初估有機會是 CoWoS 翻倍，PLP 進度比較快，PLP
規格弘塑 310*310、510*515、600*600 都有，再大到 700 以上就不
做了，晶圓代工客戶規格是 310*310 但目前進度比較慢，還在實驗
線階段。力成 510*515 進度最快，4Q26 會拉第一批機台，2027 隨
力成購置的友達廠進度持續拉機台，2028-2029 年 PLP 機台出貨量
會很明顯放大。 
 維持買進評等，目標價 3000 元：基本面無變化，預估數字也沒做調
整，故維持買進評等 ，預估 2026 年 EPS 64.81 元，2027 年 EPS 93.97
元，目標價 3000 元。(2027 PER 30x)

弘塑為本土封測設備龍頭廠商 
 弘塑為本土封測設備龍頭廠商 
弘塑為本土封測設備龍頭廠商，主要利基產品為半導體後段濕製程設備，並延
伸相關化學品、檢測設備代理及軟體解決方案。1H26 產品結構可區分為機台檢
測產品(65%)、化學產品(20%)、代理設備(15%)。2013 年合併化學品公司添鴻
(CLC)、2018 年底合併通路商佳霖(ClC)、併購資訊系統廠太引(Tyne)，投資美
國 X-Ray 檢測公司 SVXR。 客戶為國內半導體 、 光電 、記憶體及面板產業大廠，
包括台積電、矽品、日月光、力成、星科金朋、精材、聯電、茂矽、采鈺、穏
懋、中芯、宏達等。

2Q26 毛 利率 39.37%， 低於預
期，2Q26 EPS 11.18 元 
 2Q26 毛利率 39.37%，低於預期，2Q26 EPS 11.18 元 
弘塑 2Q26 EPS 11.18 元，低於元富預估的 12.34 元，獲利達成率 91%。本業
來看，2Q26 營收 17.27 億元，+8%QOQ，+6%YOY，2Q26 毛利率 39.37%，
較 1Q26 回升 5.1 個百分點，然仍低於預估的 41%，主要是外包成本及急單增
加影響部分毛利率，預期 3Q26 毛利率持續向上改善，2Q26 EPS 11.18 元。

弘塑 3Q26 營收 22.67 億元，+31.3%QOQ，+51.8%YOY，營收達成率 98%，
略低於預期，預估 3Q26 毛利率 41%，預估 3Q26 EPS 16.47 元。

營運逐季成長，OSA T展望樂觀

營運逐季成長，OSA T展望樂觀 
2026 年弘塑擴產一倍 ，1Q26 已經完成擴建 ，3Q26 新產能已經滿載。 外包部分 ，
目前外包比例 33%，未來佔比會下降，目前外包月產能一個月 5-6 台，因為外
包產能受限 ， 同業也在搶外包產能 。2027 年擴產再 50-100%， 以租用廠房為主。
2027 主要成長動能還是 OSAT，今年晶圓代工客戶營收高於 OSAT，2027 年
OSAT 營收超過晶圓代工客戶，SOIC 機台去年小量，今年量增，2027 跟 2026
差不多略增 ，2028 才會明顯大增 ， 主要還是跟隨晶圓代工客戶 SOIC 擴廠進度，
SOIC 機台種類 6-7 種。

PLP 及 CoPoS 設備機台單價很高，是 CoWoS 翻倍，PLP 進度比較快，PLP
規格弘塑 310*310、510*515、600*600 都有，再大到 700 以上就不做了，晶圓

510*515 進度最快，4Q26 會拉第一批機台，2027 隨力成購置的友達廠進度持
續拉機台，2028-2029 年 PLP 量會很明顯放大。

CPO 部分，確定會出貨的是 EIC 跟 PIC 兩個晶片 Bonding 在一起的設備，跟
SOIC 類似，PIC 的機台目前進度很慢，因為 35 族會有污染問題，Sillicon 平面
長線做濕蝕刻這塊不做，會產生治癌物。

維持買進，目標價 3000 元

維持買進評等，目標價 3000 元 
基本面無變化，預估數字也沒做調整，OSAT 拉貨樂觀，長線 SOIC 機台放量可
期，故維持買進評等，預估 2026 年 EPS 64.81 元，2027 年 EPS 93.97 元，目
標價 3000 元。(2027 PER 30x)

風險提示 
出貨不如預期，ASP 不如預期。

3Q26Re: 신규 시설 매출 인식 개시 
연결 기준 매출액 1조 4,903억원(+18.5%YoY, 이하 YoY 생략), 영업이익 6,401억원
(+1.0%, OPM 42.9%)로 컨센서스를 상회하는 실적 기록. 1~4공장이 완전 가동을 유지하는 
한편, 5공장은 PPQ 배치 생산 본격화되면서 매출 기여 확대. 상업 생산 전환이 예상되는 
2H27부터 이익 기여 커질 것으로 기대. 
연초 인수한 Rockville 공장은 3Q에도 안정적인 상업 생산을 이어갔으며 2Q 생산 물량의 
매출이 인식되며 매출 성장에 기여. 동사는 Rockville 공장 매출 인식 등을 반영하여 기존 
+15~20% 성장에서 +20%로 연간 가이던스 상향.   
고객사 수주 회복 기대 
3Q26 기준 누적 수주액은 219억 달러로 전분기 대비 소폭 증가했으나 과거 연간 수주액 
증가 대비 부족한 수준. 저조한 수주 실적은 지난해 초 의약품에 대한 관세 불확실성 증가로 
고객사들의 수주 문의가 저조했던 영향. 26년 초부터 관세 불확실성이 낮아지며 수주 문의
는 회복 추세. 약 1년 정도 논의 기간이 소요되는 것을 고려할 때 26년말~27년 초 수주 회
복 확인 가능할 것으로 기대. 
안정적인 공급망 유지가 중요한 CDMO 산업 특성상 노조 파업 가능성도 고객사 수주를 늦
추는 요인으로 판단. 다만, 10/16 인천 고등법원이 쟁의행위 가처분 항고심에 대해 인용 결
정을 하며 기존 3개 공정에서 9개 공정으로 확대되며 파업 우려는 다소 완화. 
투자 의견 Buy, 목표 주가 200만원 유지 
매수 의견과 목표 주가 200만원을 유지. 원달러 약세에도 불구하고 5공장 Ramp-up과 
Rockville 공장 매출 기여로 견조한 실적 성장 지속될 것으로 예상. 파업 이슈 해소와 대형 
수주 계약이 주가 상승을 이끌 것으로 판단.

Document 205: AI関連企業は巨額の借金を返せるのか ～データセンター投資の急拡大と高まる信用リスク～

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI関連企業は巨額の借金を返せるのか ～データセンター投資の急拡大と高まる信用リスク～.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期

### Page 1

Research Report

2026 年10 月 26 日

1/6 
AI 関連企業は巨額の借金を返せるのか 
～データセンター投資の急拡大と高まる信用リスク～ 
 資産運用調査部 主席研究員  柏村 祐

（要旨） 
○ 世界的な AI ブームが続くなか、AI 関連企業がデータセンター建設などの巨額投資を賄うた
めに社債発行や借入による資金調達を急増させている。「AI関連企業は手元資金が潤沢で財
務が盤石だ」という従来の常識は揺らぎつつあり、企業の資金不足や信用リスクに目を向け
る必要がある。 
○ AI 投資の拡大は3 つの変化を通じて、企業の財務と金融市場にリスクをもたらしうる。第一
に、テクノロジー企業の社債発行が急増し、借入依存が強まっている。第二に、巨額の設備
投資がAI大手の現金創出力（フリーキャッシュフロー）を圧迫している。第三に、信用力の
低いテクノロジー企業に対する社債市場の警戒が強まり、信用スプレッドが拡大している。 
○ 「AIの成長期待さえあれば巨額の投資もすべて回収できる」と安易に想定すべきではない。
AI 投資の資金を借入などの外部資金に頼る度合いが高まる環境では、収益化の遅れが企業の
信用力低下に直結しやすく、従来の資産配分についても再点検が必要となる。 
○ 個人投資家にとっては、保有する社債ファンドの業種偏りや信用リスクを点検することと、
株式運用において特定の AI 銘柄への過度な集中を避け、安定したキャッシュフローを生む
資産を補完的に組み入れることが、対応策の一つとなるだろう。

1. 「キャッシュリッチなAI企業」の変調 
生成AIの普及を原動力とする世界的なハイテク株高は、日本の個人投資家にとっても資産形成
の大きな追い風となってきた。NISA（少額投資非課税制度）などを通じて米国株式（S&P500な
ど）や全世界株式を保有する投資家の多くは、AI関連企業の成長や株価上昇の恩恵を受けてきた
といえる。もっとも、足元ではAI関連株の値動きが荒くなる場面も増え、巨額の投資が本当に利
益につながるのか不安を感じている投資家も少なくないだろう。一方で、市場には依然として「A
I企業の財務は盤石であり、足元の揺らぎは一時的な調整にすぎない」とみる楽観論も根強い。し
かし、企業の「台所事情」に目を向けると、これまでとは異なるリスクが見えてくる。 「AIブー
ム」と聞くと、華やかな技術革新や株価の上昇ばかりに目が向きがちだが、企業財務の視点に立
つと、巨額のデータセンター投資が企業の手元資金を圧迫し、一部のAI関連企業では借入などの

### Page 2

2/6 
外部資金への依存度が高まりつつある。 
華やかなAI株高の裏側で膨らむ巨額の借金を、AI関連企業は本当に返せるのだろうか。 「テクノ
ロジー企業は潤沢な現金を持ち、財務が健全だ」という従来の常識にとらわれていると、資金不
足や信用リスクの拡大という構造的な変化を見落としかねない。本稿では、AI投資の拡大が引き
起こす 3つの変化として、 「社債発行の急増」 「現金創出力の圧迫」 「信用スプレッドの拡大」を整
理し、個人投資家が資産運用において注意すべき論点を提示したい。なお、本稿では、AI投資を
主導する巨大テクノロジー企業（ハイパースケーラー）から、AI関連事業を手掛ける中堅企業ま
でを幅広く「AI関連企業」と総称する。また、 「テクノロジー企業」は、社債発行統計などにおい
てテクノロジー（IT）部門に分類される企業を指す用語として用いる。

2. AI投資の拡大はなぜ資金不足と信用リスクを招くのか 
(1) AI投資の拡大で急増するテクノロジー企業の社債発行 
現在のAIブームを牽引する巨大テクノロジー企業（ハイパースケーラー）をはじめとするAI関
連企業は、データセンターの建設や先端半導体の確保、電力インフラの整備などに巨額の資金を
投じている。これまでテクノロジー企業は、株式発行や潤沢な手元資金を主な資金源としてきた
が、投資規模の急拡大に伴い、資金調達のあり方が大きく変わりつつある。 
RBA（豪州準備銀行）が2026 年10月に公表した「Financial Stability Review」によれば、AI
関連企業は大規模な投資計画を賄うため、社債やプライベート・クレジット、銀行借入など、負
債による資金調達への依存を強めている。資料1の上段は米国の投資適格社債の発行総額に占め
るテクノロジー部門の割合を、下段はハイパースケーラー（巨大クラウド・AI企業）とテクノロ
ジー部門の実際の社債発行額を示したものである。2026年には、米国の投資適格社債の発行総額
に占めるテクノロジー部門の割合が約27％に達している。また、下段が示すように、足元ではハ
イパースケーラーによる起債が急増し、部門全体の社債発行額を大きく押し上げている。RBAは、
今後計画されているAI関連の設備投資のうち3分の1以上が負債性資金によって賄われるとの市
場の見方を紹介している。つまり、AIブームが続くほど、テクノロジー企業の借入依存が強ま
り、公募・私募の債券市場全体がAI投資の成否に左右されやすくなるという新たな構図が生まれ
ている。

### Page 3

3/6 
資料 1 テクノロジー部門の社債発行が急増、米国投資適格社債の発行総額に占める割合は約 27％に 
（注）米国の非金融企業による投資適格社債の発行額（期間は 2010 年 3 月～2026 年 7 月 17 日）。テクノロジー部門にはコンピ
ュータ・電子機器を含む。上段の割合は 4 四半期移動平均。ハイパースケーラーは Alphabet、Amazon、Meta、Microsoft、
Oracle、NVIDIA を指す。 
（出所）Reserve Bank of Australia, “Financial Stability Review – October 2026”をもとに第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

(2) 巨額の設備投資が企業の現金創出力を圧迫 
なぜ、高い収益力を誇る巨大テクノロジー企業までもが借入への依存を強めているのか。その
背景には、データセンター構築などの設備投資があまりにも巨額であり、本業の稼ぐ力（営業利
益）だけでは投資資金を賄いきれなくなっている現実がある。 
IMF（国際通貨基金）が2026 年10月に公表した「Global Financial Stability Report」は、
AI投資の中心を担う主要ハイパースケーラー5社（Alphabet、Amazon、Meta、Microsoft、
Oracle）の財務状況を分析している。資料2は、これら 5社の設備投資（Capex：Capital 
Expenditure） 、営業利益、およびフリーキャッシュフローの推移を示したものである。5社の設備
投資は 2023年頃から急拡大し、2025年後半から2026年にかけて営業利益を上回る規模に達して
いる。その結果、フリーキャッシュフロー（右軸）は急減し、2023～24年のピーク時には四半期
ベースで600 億米ドルを超えていたものが、2026年にはほぼゼロまで落ち込んでいる。もっと
も、ハイパースケーラー各社は現時点では強固なバランスシートと十分な流動性を維持してい
る。しかし、激しい競争圧力や技術の陳腐化に対応するため、企業が投資の手を緩めることは難
しい。今後、AIの収益化が市場の期待を下回るようなことがあれば、企業の現金創出力は一段と
圧迫され、追加の資金調達や財務負担の増大を招く可能性がある。

### Page 4

4/6

資料 2 主要ハイパースケーラー5 社の設備投資が急増し、2026 年のフリーキャッシュフローはほぼゼロに 
（注）主要ハイパースケーラー5 社（Alphabet、Amazon、Meta、Microsoft、Oracle）の設備投資（Capex、左軸）、営業利益（左軸）、
およびフリーキャッシュフロー（右軸）の四半期ごとの推移（単位：10 億米ドル）。 
（出所）IMF, “Global Financial Stability Report, October 2026”をもとに第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

(3) 信用力の低いテクノロジー企業に対する社債市場の警戒 
さらに、AI投資への借入依存が高まるなか、社債市場ではテクノロジー企業の信用リスクに対
する警戒が強まりつつある。株式市場ではAIの成長期待が注目されやすいが、元利金の確実な返
済を重視する債券市場では、企業の財務体力やビジネスモデルの持続可能性に対する選別がシビ
アに進んでいる。 
ECB（欧州中央銀行）が2026 年5月に公表した「Financial Stability Review」では、AIを巡
る懸念が株式市場だけでなく社債市場にも波及していると指摘している。資料3 は、2025年 3月
から 2026年5 月にかけてのユーロ圏および米国におけるハイイールド（HY、低格付け）社債の信
用スプレッドの推移を示したものである。米国・ユーロ圏ともに、ハイイールド債市場全体（米
国HY、ユーロ圏HY）のスプレッドが300ベーシスポイント（bp）前後で比較的落ち着いて推移し
ていたのに対し、信用力の低いテクノロジー企業（米国HYテック、ユーロ圏HYテック）のスプレ
ッドは 2026年に入り500bp超へと大きく拡大し、同年 5月時点でも450bp前後と、市場全体を上
回る水準で推移していた。 
ECBは、AIが既存のソフトウェア企業などのビジネスモデルを脅かすリスクに加え、内部資金だ
けでなく借入に頼ってAI投資を拡大させる動きが金融安定上の懸念になりうると指摘している。
巨額のAI投資ブームの裏側では、信用力の低いテクノロジー企業に対する市場の警戒が強まり、
企業の借入依存が信用市場にも新たなリスクをもたらしつつある。 「借りた巨額の資金を本当に返

### Page 5

5/6 
せるのか」という市場の問いかけがすでに始まっているのである。

資料 3 信用力の低いテクノロジー企業の信用スプレッドはハイイールド債市場全体より大きく拡大 
（注）2025 年 3 月 1 日～2026 年 5 月 19 日におけるユーロ圏および米国のハイイールド（HY）社債の信用スプレッド（OIS カーブ 
に対する上乗せ金利、時価総額加重平均、単位：ベーシスポイント）。「HY テック」はハイイールド格付けのテクノロジー企業
を指す。 
縦の破線は左から 2025 年 4 月の米国の関税発表、前回 FSR（金融安定報告書）公表時点（2025 年 11 月）、中東戦争の開 
始（2026 年 3 月初旬）を示す。 
（出所）European Central Bank, “Financial Stability Review, May 2026”をもとに第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

3. 「AI借金時代」の資産防衛術 
第 2 章で見てきたように、AI ブームの裏側では「社債発行の急増」 「現金創出力の圧迫」 「信用
スプレッドの拡大」という3 つの変化が同時に進行している。この視点に立てば、AIブームが抱
える死角は「株価の割高感」だけでは説明できない。巨大テクノロジー企業から中堅のテクノロ
ジー企業に至るまで、AI投資の拡大に伴い、一部の企業では自己資金だけでなく、借入などの外
部資金への依存度が高まりつつある。 
ここで重要なのは、 「テクノロジー企業はキャッシュリッチで財務が盤石だ」と安易に考えない
ことである。仮にAI の収益化が遅れれば、膨らんだ借入負担が企業の業績や信用力を直撃するシ
ナリオも視野に入れる必要がある。こうした「AI借金時代」を迎えるなか、個人投資家には、従
来のポートフォリオに潜む信用リスクを改めて点検することが求められる。

### Page 6

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
6/6 
第一に、社債ファンドやハイイールド債（低格付け社債）における「信用リスク」を過小評価
しないことである。2026年の米国の投資適格社債の発行総額に占めるテクノロジー部門の割合が
約27％に達し、信用力の低いテクノロジー企業のスプレッドが拡大している現状では、 「債券だか
ら安心」 「株安を社債がカバーしてくれる」という従来の前提が働きにくい可能性がある。AI の収
益期待が後退する局面では、AI 関連株の下落と信用スプレッドの拡大（社債価格の下落）が同時
に進みかねない点に注意が必要だ。債券を保有する場合には、表面的な利回りの高さだけでハイ
イールド債や社債ファンドを選好するのではなく、組入資産の業種の偏りを確認することや、信
用リスクの低い先進国国債などへの分散を検討することも、選択肢の一つとなる。 
第二に、株式運用において「AI関連だから買う」という一括りの発想から脱却し、企業の現金
創出力と業種分散を重視することである。NISAなどを活用してS&P500や全世界株式連動商品を保
有する投資家は多いが、これらの指数では、米国の巨大テクノロジー企業の構成比率が高まって
おり、特にS&P500では一部の企業への集中が進んでいる。指数連動商品をポートフォリオの中核
に据えつつも、補完的な資産として、安定したフリーキャッシュフローを生み出す高配当バリュ
ー株やディフェンシブ株、あるいはAIデータセンター投資の受け皿となる電力・インフラ関連株
などを組み入れることは、AI 企業の資金不足や淘汰の波に備える選択肢の一つとなるだろう。た
だし、特定のテーマや業種に偏りすぎないよう、投資比率には十分な注意が必要である。 
AI 革命は株式市場に大きな成長機会をもたらす一方で、企業の財務構造や信用市場のリスクを
も変えつつある。これからは、 「AIだから成長する」という発想にとどまらず、その裏側で膨らむ
「借金の行方」まで見据えることが重要になる。AI 企業のフリーキャッシュフローや信用スプレ
ッドの動きも、株価と同様に注視する。それが、巨額のAI投資時代において投資家に求められる
新たな視点であろう。

以 上

招股⽂件显⽰，公司已背负 62 亿美元流动债务，未启动租赁承诺更从 500 亿美元暴增⾄
3210 亿美元，还潜藏 770 亿美元额外借款选项。其依托博通构建的表外 " 芯⽚融资 " 结构，
更被视为循环交易对⼿⻛险隐患。对⽐ SpaceX 明确承诺投资级并顺利完成 250 亿美元债券
发⾏， Anthropic 对投资级信⽤评级⼏乎只字未提态度令债市买家难以定价。
摘要：
Anthropic 在备受瞩⽬的 IPO 招股⽂件中，对外界最关⼼的投资级信⽤评级问题⼏乎只字未提，
却同时警告可能⽐预期更早寻求新⼀轮融资。这⼀组合信号，正在让债券市场的潜在买家陷⼊
观望。
招股⽂件显⽰，截⾄今年 6 ⽉ 30 ⽇， Anthropic 已有 62 亿美元的流动债务，全部以可转换本票形
式⽋于亚⻢逊。与此同时，公司还披露了⼀笔由摩根⼠丹利牵头、规模⾼达 150 亿美元的五年期
循环信贷额度，以及从亚⻢逊、 Broadcom 和⾕歌⺟公司 Alphabet 处再借 770 亿美元可转换票据
的潜在选项。更令市场瞩⽬的是，截⾄ 6 ⽉底， Anthropic 尚未启动的租赁承诺 —— 主要与算⼒和

基础设施容量相关 —— 已从去年 12 ⽉底的约 500 亿美元骤升⾄ 3210 亿美元。
债务规模的急剧膨胀，使投资级评级的重要性愈发凸显。⼀旦获得投资级评级， Anthropic 将能
以更低成本进⼊保险公司、养⽼基⾦等⼤型机构投资者主导的⾼评级债券市场，从⽽打开更深
厚的资本池。然⽽，穆迪、标普和惠誉⽬前均尚未发布对 Anthropic 的评级。
招股⽂件对投资级评级态度暧昧
与同为科技独⻆兽的 SpaceX 相⽐， Anthropic 在投资级评级问题上的表态明显更为保守。据彭博
报道， SpaceX 在今年早些时候准备上市时，在招股⽂件中明确表⽰ " 致⼒于维持投资级信⽤评
级 " ，并随即获得穆迪、标普和惠誉三家机构的投资级认定，随后在 6 ⽉完成了⼀笔 250 亿美元的
投资级债券交易。
Anthropic 的招股⽂件则并未如此清晰地勾勒出投资级路径。这⼀态度上的差异，令债券市场潜
在买家难以形成明确预期，也使外界对其信⽤质量的判断更加复杂。
尽管措辞审慎，招股⽂件中的部分条款仍透露出 Anthropic 对投资级评级的隐性诉求。

那笔 150 亿美元的循环信贷额度 —— 由摩根⼠丹利牵头，于 9 ⽉完成，规模较此前约 30 亿美元⼤
幅扩容 —— 包含⼀项关键条款：若 Anthropic 获得⾄少两家主要评级机构的投资级认定，相关担
保和抵押权益将予以解除。
此外，该信贷额度的利率将随评级⾼低浮动： A 级评级对应基准利率上浮 75 个基点，⽽最低投资
级 BBB- 则对应上浮 125 个基点。这意味着，评级越⾼， Anthropic 的融资成本越低，激励机制清
晰可⻅。
资产负债表：流动性充裕，但承诺规模惊⼈
据报道，市场对 Anthropic 信⽤状况的判断，核⼼⽭盾在于：当前杠杆率尚在可控范围，但未来
承诺规模已令⼈咋⾆。

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
• Central banks’ communication is carefully scrutinised for
every nuance. Lately, there seems to have been more RBA
communication to pore over. The method of delivery has
also evolved. This has sometimes caused confusion when
tone seems to vary. Different audiences call for different

tone and varying complexity.
• The content has evolved, too. RBA officials are increasingly
using speeches directed to professional audiences to
discuss more technical estimates of inferred variables such
as neutral rates and financial conditions. They are also
highlighting that monetary policy combats inflation in more
ways than just squeezing mortgage holders.
• One thing has not changed: the RBA is not in the business
of providing ‘forward guidance’ promises about the future

path of interest rates. It does, however, give more running
commentary about how it is interpreting recent data. These
interpretations are themselves useful signals about what
the RBA thinks it might do.
Market participants pore over every utterance by central banks
for clues to future rate moves. They will have plenty to pore over
in the run-up to the November RBA decision. After the flurry
of fireside chats this week, ’60 Minutes’ will run a pre-recorded

interview with chief economist Sarah Hunter on Sunday. Next
Wednesday, the whole senior policy team will appear before
Senate Estimates, just as the quarterly CPI is released. (This
timing will have been the Senate’s choice, not the RBA’s.) After
the decision will come the Statement on Monetary Policy, a
media release and press conference. More speeches will follow,
including one by the Deputy Governor on the Thursday.
RBA communication does seem to have changed, not just

following the RBA Review but also more recently. There may
be fewer policy meetings each year, but the communication
outside that certainly feels more frequent than a couple of years
ago. Back in 2024, speaking engagements felt a little sparse
outside the post-meeting media conference and parliamentary
appearances. We have counted 58 public engagements so
far this year on the Speeches page on the RBA’s website.
This compares with 58 for the whole of last year and 52 in

2024. Excluding the ‘compulsory’ press conferences and
parliamentary appearances, fewer than half of the 2024 events
were related to economics, markets and monetary policy.
The ‘how’ has changed
The way the RBA communicates with markets has changed
as well. There are fewer set-piece speeches on policy and a
Open mouth operations
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
lot more semi-scripted ‘fireside chats’. Compared with a few
years ago, there are more interviews across a wide range of

media. This is a big change from the pre-Review world. It was
a huge deal when the previous Governor appeared on the
7:30 Report ; it is hard to imagine him agreeing to an Assistant
Governor appearing on 60 Minutes.
Part of the change reflects increased efforts to reach a general
audience. Central banks want to explain why they do what
they do. This matters especially when interest rates are rising
but the household sector is not the source of the inflation.

Speaking differently to different audiences can sometimes
confuse markets. As the RBA’s Chief Communication Officer
highlighted in a recent panel discussion, the post-meeting
media conference is designed to speak to the public. Market
participants and economists certainly tune in, but we are not
the primary audience. So, if you think that what the Governor
said in the media conference sounded more ‘dovish’ than
what another official says in a more market-oriented forum,

it is partly because she is trying to convey that the RBA
understands that its recent policy actions are making things
harder for many Australians. But this is in much the same way
that a doctor might say, “This might hurt a bit.” It does, but that
will not stop the treatment.
A related issue is that it is easier to control tone in pre-drafted
material such as the minutes and set-piece speeches than in
unstructured Q&A. Senior central bankers are trained to be

careful with their words in unscripted settings. If the tone
differs, the minutes are still more likely to say what the Board
really means than an off-the-cuff answer.
Greater communication with the broader public might have
reduced the pressure to make the set-piece speeches more
accessible. Research on central bank communication often
uses measures such as the Flesch–Kincaid Grade Level and
Flesch Reading Ease score. A comprehensive analysis was

beyond the scope of this note. However, a spot comparison
suggests four speeches from 2026 ( 1, 2, 3, 4) were harder
to read than four from 2021 (1, 2, 3, 4). The 2021 speeches
scored in the 11 to 12 range, which is high-school-level
“The RBA does not want to promise what it will
do, but it is increasingly trying to tell us what
it thinks. "

### Page 3

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
3
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
complexity. The 2026 speeches scored between 12 and
15, which is academic or technically dense text requiring
university-level education. (Including two of my own 2021
speeches is an obvious caveat, but the two speeches by then-
Governor Phil Lowe also scored better on these metrics than

the sample from this year. It should be noted, though, that
readability metrics do not necessarily imply better clarity if the
concepts discussed are more abstract.)
The ‘what’ has changed
As noted above, the content of RBA communication has
evolved, too. As its intellectual framework has begun to put
more weight on the balance of supply and demand, and those
invisible ‘star’ variables (NAIRUs, neutral real rates, ‘financial
conditions’, etc.), the communication to professional audiences

has increasingly focused on those topics.
More recently, the RBA has also placed more emphasis on
the full range of transmission channels from interest rates to
inflation (though not, as we have noted previously, the labour
supply channel). This is understandable in a world of supply
shocks. People need to understand that monetary policy is not
simply an exercise in hitting mortgage borrowers. We doubt
they would be comforted, though, by the Deputy Governor’s

explanation this week that the ‘cashflow channel’ was the
smallest of the transmission channels from interest rates
to inflation, with the exchange rate much more prominent.
That implies a lot of pain for a small group, for little effect on
inflation. It raises the question of whether other policy tools
or institutional arrangements might get the same effect on
inflation through the other channels without hitting a minority
of households disproportionately.

We have also seen a move away from using catchphrases
to summarise the position. From ‘narrow path’ to ‘not
ruling anything in or out’ to ‘cautious and predictable’, once
entrenched, a phrase can outlast its usefulness.
One thing that has not changed is the way the minutes record
the presentations to the Board and the members’ discussion. The
minutes rarely convey much new information beyond the post-
meeting statements. Sometimes, though, the minutes reveal the

Board’s reaction to the staff’s analysis. The August 2024 minutes
were a good example: the discussion highlighted just how
sensitive the forecasts were to key judgements by the staff.
The ‘why’ has not changed
Despite these changes, the underlying objectives of the RBA’s
communication have not changed. It wants to explain – and
perhaps justify – its decisions. And it wants to describe how
it interprets incoming data, especially inflation, the labour

market and the balance of supply and demand.
The RBA, like any central bank, is not really in the business
of providing ‘forward guidance’ about future moves.
Circumstances change, so it can never guarantee a particular
future path for interest rates. Some academic literature argues
that promising to keep rates low for a long time (or high,
for that matter) independently amplifies the transmission
of monetary policy beyond the effect of current-period

interest rates alone, by shifting the short end of the yield
curve. Central banks including the RBA have certainly tried
jawboning in this way from time to time. The prevailing view,
though, is that the potential cost of reputational loss if things
do not turn out as predicted outweighs the benefits of a
slightly stronger transmission mechanism.
In this context, it is the interpretation of data that provides
the signal about future policy directions. This is why it was

noteworthy that the Governor still characterised the labour
market as ‘a little tight’ after the September meeting, even
though the Board’s post-meeting communication did not. We
have been noting for some time that the RBA staff have a
downbeat view of supply capacity. Together with the RBA’s
oft-stated concerns about inflation, this is why the Deputy
Governor’s ‘hawkishness’ did not surprise us.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: managing expectations
While annual retail sales growth improved in September,
rising from 0.4%yr to 1.1%yr, the current pace of growth
is only a quarter of the gain seen just a year ago and very
weak versus history. Consumer spending continues to be

constrained by weak confidence, particularly with respect to
employment prospects and wealth. On housing wealth, five
years into the property downturn, house prices continue to
decline month-on-month and investment remains in a deep
downturn, –20.6%ytd.
Total fixed asset investment has been detracting from
annual GDP growth since April owing to a lull in high-tech
investment, a lack of confidence in future demand amongst
domestically oriented businesses as well as property’s

malaise. For aggregate growth to hold up through 2027 and
2028 as the contribution from net exports moderates, broad-
based stimulus in scale will prove necessary. Expect recent
targeted support measures to be broadened and given much
greater scale in coming months.
Finally, in the UK, inflation accelerated from 3.1%yr to 3.5%yr
in September, exceeding both market expectations and the
Bank of England's July forecast. The surprise was largely
driven by energy, fuel prices rising 5.4%mth. However, core

inflation also edged up from 2.6%yr to 2.8%yr as services
inflation increased from 3.4%yr to 3.7%yr. While not an
immediate concern inflation, the labour market showing
resilience, the ILO unemployment rate falling from 4.9% to
4.7% in August, suggests demand risks for inflation will need
close monitoring as well as supply-side concerns.
Next week will see the focus once again return to the US as
the FOMC meet and US Q3 GDP is released. For both events,

the implications for risks to the outlook will be the focus. The
week after, the US mid-term elections are due.
With little domestic data of significance, participants’
attention focused on RBA Deputy Governor Hauser's fireside
chat, which saw an abrupt shift in market expectations of a
follow-up rate hike at the November meeting.
Hauser warned in his remarks that the current inflation outlook
was "materially worse" than the RBA's August forecasts.

Three upside inflation risks were characterised as having
"crystallised" ahead of the September meeting – the Middle
East, the AI boom, and domestic supply constraints. Hauser
noted the Middle East picture "looks worse" given oil prices
are "about a third higher than [the RBA] assumed in August”.
He then positioned AI and data centre developments among
an "extraordinary sequence of positive demand shocks around
the world", with "higher AI prices, tech prices feeding through

into CPI". Finally, on domestic capacity pressures, the Deputy
Governor noted that "inflation's been coming in at or a little
bit above expectations, despite an apparent easing in the
labour market". He went on to state that the staff are currently
assessing "whether [the RBA] need to change [its] judgement
on capacity pressures in the labour market again" – in other
words, revise up their assumption for the NAIRU.
Hauser's comments prompted a material reassessment of

market pricing for a follow-up November hike, from just a
20% chance at the end of last week to a 50/50 proposition
today ahead of next week's Q3 CPI. For that release, we are
forecasting a 1.3% (4.1%yr) lift in headline inflation and a
0.9 (3.5%yr) increase in trimmed mean inflation. Our preview
provides more detail behind the component mix for Q3 as
well as our upward revisions to the preliminary Q4 forecast.
Provided inflation prints broadly in line with our expectations,

the RBA is likely to hike by 25bp at the November meeting.
Across in New Zealand, consumer prices rose 0.7% in Q3
and annual inflation eased from 4.1%yr to 3.7%yr. Under
the surface of this ‘as expected’ read though, several closely
watched measures of core inflation took a step higher to now
be at or slightly above the top of the RBNZ’s target band. Our
NZ economics team consequently expect the RBNZ will raise
the OCR 25bps to 3.00% at its October policy review, while

retaining a 4.00% terminal OCR forecast for September 2027.
Earlier in the week, China's economy surprised  to the upside in
Q3, GDP growth accelerating from 0.9%qtr to 1.2%qtr, lifting
annual growth from 4.3%yr to 4.5%yr – keeping authorities on
track to achieve their 4.5–5.0% full-year growth objective.
Elliot Clarke, Head of International Economics
Ryan Wells, Economist
Illiana Jain, Economist

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
RBNZ now expected to hike in October
We now expect the RBNZ will deliver a 25bp hike at its
policy review on 28 October. While September quarter
inflation was slightly below the RBNZ’s forecast, the
underlying detail was firm. More recently, increases in

fuel prices point to a material lift in inflation by year-end.
We expect further interest rate increases over the coming
months, with the OCR reaching a peak of 4% next year.
Softer headline inflation but firm core pressures
leave RBNZ on course to hike in October
We now expect the RBNZ to raise the Official Cash Rate
by 25bp at its October policy review (previously, we had
expected a pause at this meeting). This change in view reflects
significant upside risks to near-term inflation relative to the

RBNZ’s previous forecast.
While in line with our forecast, the September quarter inflation
figures were slightly softer than the RBNZ expected, with prices
rising 3.7% over the past year (compared with the RBNZ’s
forecast of 3.9%). However, the details were less reassuring.
Excluding volatile items such as food and fuel, underlying
inflation pressures remained firm. Closely watched measures
of core inflation moved higher and now sit close to, or a little

above, the top of the RBNZ’s target band. We also saw an
increase in the number of goods recording price rises, taking
that measure back to levels seen in the wake of the pandemic.
Alongside the existing firmness in underlying inflation, the
risks for inflation over the coming quarters are firmly tilted to
the upside. That near-term upside risk has also been noted by
the Governor. Following a sharp rise in global fuel costs, we
expect inflation to reach 4.3% by the end of this year (above

the RBNZ’s September MPS forecast for +3.9%). While that is
mainly due to higher prices at the pump, businesses in sectors
such as construction are also reporting related increases in
transport and materials costs. Consistent with those trends,
we expect core inflation to remain in the upper part of the
RBNZ’s target band through 2027.
Reinforcing the case for an OCR hike at the October meeting,
the New Zealand dollar is notably weaker than the RBNZ

assumed: on a trade-weighted basis, the NZD is currently
3.6% below the RBNZ’s September projections. If sustained,
that would add to inflation pressures over the next few
quarters. We suspect this will not have shifted the RBNZ’s
medium-term outlook for inflation.
Several other developments need to be weighed against that
firmer inflation outlook. First, wholesale long-term interest rates
have risen markedly, and there has also been a more moderate

rise in some mortgage rates. The RBNZ is likely to note the role of
those increases in managing medium-term inflation risks.
In addition, the RBNZ is likely to continue to emphasise that
there is little evidence of a lift in businesses’ longer-term plans
for price setting at this stage in response to the rise in energy
prices (sometimes referred to as “second-round inflation
effects”). We expect the RBNZ to highlight the risk that more
persistent inflation pressures could emerge, depending on the

duration of the oil price shock.
We also expect the RBNZ to note that the labour market
remains cyclically weak and is not a significant driver of inflation.
Employment growth that remains weak relative to labour force
growth, subdued growth in job vacancies and low labour market
turnover are consistent with this view and could be emphasised.
Finally, the RBNZ is likely to acknowledge that while the
global growth outlook remains solid, there are significant

downside risks from the Iran war and concerns about the
sustainability of the AI investment cycle.
Balancing those concerns, we think the MPC will lift the
cash rate next week. However, a vote seems very possible as
differences in view will likely remain among the MPC about
how much the medium-term inflation outlook has really
shifted in recent weeks.
In September, the bulk of the MPC saw upside risks to the
inflation outlook while two members (Conway and Hansen)

thought the risks were balanced. Recent developments will
not have made those seeing upside risks to inflation more
Satish Ranchhod
Senior Economist

### Page 6

6
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
comfortable, while the more dovish MPC members will
likely focus on the ongoing downside risks for growth given
continued softness in house prices and the mounting pressure
on real incomes. Indeed, all MPC members saw some degree
of downside risks to growth which likely remain relevant. All

these messages should remain in the RBNZ’s updated views.
The MPC is likely to continue to present its decision as a
gradual evolution of the approach taken since July: a higher
OCR now provides insurance against the need for a much
higher OCR later if upside risks to medium-term inflation
materialise. We doubt the aim will be to push market interest
rates materially higher.
RBNZ to leave door open for further hikes, OCR
still expected to peak at 4% in 2027

In its forward guidance, the RBNZ is likely to leave open the option
of raising the OCR further at the December Monetary Policy
Statement, without presenting an increase as certain. While the
RBNZ will not publish an updated OCR projection at this review,
the press statement is likely to note that further increases may
be needed, depending on economic conditions. The September
projection showed the OCR eventually rising to 3.25%.
We expect the RBNZ will end up delivering another 25bp

hike in December, followed by a further hike in February.
That would take the OCR to 3.5%, roughly where the RBNZ
estimates the neutral rate to be. At that point, we expect the
RBNZ to slow the pace of rate increases (previously, we had
expected that point to be reached a little later).
We continue to expect further 25bp hikes at the June and
September 2027 meetings, taking the OCR to a peak of 4.0%.
While oil prices are generating significant swings in inflation,

underlying pressures remain firm. More restrictive conditions are
likely to be needed to bring core inflation sustainably back to 2%.
Scenarios
Our central scenario, with a probability of around 60%, is that
the RBNZ increases the OCR by 25bp and signals that a further
increase in December remains an option, without committing to
a markedly tighter future stance than it outlined in September.
Alternative scenarios include:
• A hawkish scenario (around a 20% probability) would see

the OCR increase to 3%, with the RBNZ placing greater
emphasis on upside risks to core and medium-term inflation.
That would suggest an upward revision to the RBNZ’s
projected OCR path in December. Downside risks to the
exchange rate could also be emphasised.
• A dovish scenario (around a 20% probability) would
see the RBNZ leave the OCR unchanged and signal that
the medium-term inflation outlook has not changed
meaningfully from its September forecast, despite higher

near-term inflation. In this scenario, the RBNZ would
reiterate its previous forecast: a 25bp increase in December
and one further hike during 2027.
How might the election play into the decision?
While next week’s meeting is close to the General Election
on 7 November, the decision taken by the MPC will not be
politically motivated. However, the election outcome is close.
And should it result in a Labour Party-led coalition, it seems
likely the new government would adopt the Labour Party’s

promise to restore the RBNZ’s dual mandate. That would
require the MPC to weigh the degree of labour market slack
more highly in its deliberations. As the degree of labour market
slack currently seems high, a new mandate might justify the
MPC taking more time to return inflation to the middle of the
target range (our earlier note discusses this issue). This could
figure into the deliberations of some MPC members if the
decision between leaving the OCR unchanged and raising it

now is viewed as finely balanced.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUS: Sep Private Sector Credit (%mth)
Oct 30, Last: 0.6, Westpac f/c: 0.5
Market f/c: 0.5, Range: 0.4 to 0.6
Private sector credit growth came in slightly firmer than
expected at 0.6%mth in August, above Westpac’s and the
market’s forecast for a 0.5%mth gain, leaving credit growth

running at 8.4%yr. Growth in housing credit continued to ease,
as we expected, though the mix highlights that the waning in
momentum is uneven, centred on investors rather than owner-
occupiers. Business credit meanwhile remains a source of
strength, notwithstanding some slowing recently.
For September, we expect credit growth to nudge to 0.5%mth,
with the divergence between housing credit and business
credit persisting.
AUS: Q3/Sep CPI (%qtr/%mth)

Oct 28, Quarter, Last: 0.6%, Westpac f/c: 1.3%,
Market f/c: 1.3%, Range: 0.6 to 1.4.
Oct 28, Month, Last: 0.4%, Westpac f/c: 0.6%,
Market f/c: 0.6 Range: 0.3 to 0.8
As noted in the review of the August CPI, our September
quarter CPI estimate is unchanged at 1.3%qtr holding the
annual pace flat at 3.9%yr. At the margin there was a 0.04ppt
increase due to a lift in auto fuel but it still rounds to 1.3%.
The seasonally adjusted CPI gains 1.1% highlighting that part

of the increase is due to usual seasonality.
In the month, the September CPI is expected to rise 0.6% with
the annual pace lifting to 4.1%yr. Recreation & culture make
the largest contribution but transport is not far behind. Our
seasonally adjusted estimate increases 0.5%.
For more details please see “ September CPI Preview “.
AUS: Q3/Sep Trimmed Mean (%qtr/mth)
Oct 28, Quarter, Last: 0.8%, Westpac f/c: 0.9%,
Market f/c: 1.0%, Range: 0.9 to 1.0.

Oct 28, Month, Last: 0.2%, Westpac f/c: 0.3%,
Market f/c: 0.3%, Range: 0.2 to 0.4
Our September quarter Trimmed Mean estimate is unchanged
at 0.9%qtr with the annual pace dipping a touch from 3.6%yr
to 3.5%yr. There was a small downward revision of 0.03ppt to
0.88% compared to our original published estimate but it still
rounds to 0.9%qtr.
In the month of September we are looking for a 0.3% in
the Trimmed Mean holding the annual pace steady at 3.6%

which will be the fourth consecutive month at this pace. The
distribution of the September Trimmed Mean ranges from
–0.3% to 0.7%.
For more details please see “ September CPI Preview “.

NZ: RBNZ Official Cash Rate Review (%)
Oct 28, Last: 2.75, Westpac f/c: 3.00, Mkt f/c: 3.00
We expect the RBNZ to raise the OCR by 25bps to 3% at
the 28 October Review. Data since September show a higher
outlook for year-end inflation and a stronger GDP starting

point, but increased risks to domestic consumption and
residential investment. On balance, the medium-term inflation
picture will probably be judged to have worsened slightly
given recent movements in energy prices and the exchange
rate. A vote seems very possible as differences in view will
likely remain among the MPC about how much the medium-
term inflation outlook has really shifted in recent weeks.
Forward guidance will likely leave open the option to raise

the OCR further at the December Monetary Policy Statement
without presenting it as a sure thing.
NZ: Oct ANZ Business Confidence (index)
Oct 29, Last: 51.9
Firms remained optimistic in the September survey of business
confidence, with both the activity and inflation measures
broadly holding steady. However, it’s likely that most responses
were submitted in the early part of the month, while fuel prices
took a sharp step higher from the second half of September. The

survey comes too late for the RBNZ’s October policy review, but
evidence about the breadth of cost and price pressures could
have a bearing on the RBNZ’s thinking about the total amount of
policy tightening needed in the months ahead.
NZ: Sep Monthly Employment Indicator (%mth)
Oct 28, Last: 0.2, Westpac f/c: 0.3
The MEI has been gradually ticking higher in recent months,
showing that the jobs market has held its ground through
the Iran conflict and the consequent surge in fuel prices. The

weekly snapshots provided by Stats NZ have been running
ahead of year-ago levels and suggest another solid result for
September – we’ve pencilled in a 0.3% increase. Given the
history of this measure, however, both this month and the
previous month are likely to be revised down a little from their
initial release.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
Oct FOMC meeting
Oct 27-28, Last: 3.875%, Mkt f/c: 3.875%, WBC f/c: 3.875%
Core CPI inflation and producer price inflation both surprised
to the upside in September, and the price of Brent oil has
risen back to around USD110. Underlying momentum in
the economy also remains robust, although employment

disappointed in the latest update. Given the concerns shown
by Committee members over price risks and their collective
resolve to return annual inflation to target, the above data
could easily justify a follow-up hike at the October meeting.
That said, Committee members who have spoken recently
have shown patience with respect to assessing the economy
and positioing policy. The market has responded, effectively
pricing out the chance of an October hike in favour of a

December move, which is also our expectation.
US Q3 GDP
Oct 29, %ann’d, Last: 2.2%, Mkt f/c: 3.0%, WBC f/c: 3.1%
The US economy continues to grow at a pace well above trend.
This outperformance is almost entirely due to the strength of
investment in data centres and other AI related assets. The
pipeline for this activity remains strong, limiting near-term
risks to the downside.
Abstracting from AI investment’s contribution, other business
investment and residential investment is weak, weighed down

by high term interest rates and uncertainty related to trade
policy. Household consumption growth is volatile month-to-
month, but the trend is solid. Absent a marked deterioration
in the labour market, which is not our expectation, this
momentum is likely to persist, as wealth continues to grow and
household income gains neutralises inflation. Come 2027 and
2028, growth is expected to slow to trend, but not below.

Document 260: Entering the age of the older consumer As America ages, older consumers have more leisure time, significant wealth and growing economic influence. 2 min read

Source PDF: all report pdf/Entering the age of the older consumer As America ages, older consumers have more leisure time, significant wealth and growing economic influence. 2 min read.pdf
Report source: Bank of America
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 8
Status: ok

### Page 1

13023135     1

Economy 
Entering the age of the older consumer 
01 October 2026

Key takeaways 
• The older consumer is moving center stage. America's population has been aging for decades, but the shift has accelerated: the 
share aged over 60 increased by almost 10 percentage points (pp) between 1995 and 2025. And the Census Bureau projects the 
share of the population over 60 will rise another 5pp by 2055, to stand at nearly 30% of the population. 
• More free time means a different spending mix. People aged 65 and over spend around two and a half fewer hours per day 
working and roughly two hours more on leisure than the overall adult population. Bank of America data shows that older 
households allocate relatively more of their card spending to groceries and travel, and less to restaurants, gasoline, general 
merchandise and clothing. 
• Households aged over 55 held close to $140 trillion in net worth in the second quarter of 2026, giving many older consumers 
considerable capacity to spend on travel and other leisure categories. But wealth is not evenly distributed, and healthcare and 
other cost pressures remain significant for older households on tighter or fixed incomes.

Population aging is nothing new, but it is accelerating  
The US population has been growing relatively “older” for a long time. Exhibit 1 shows that the proportion of the population 
under 20 years old shrank from the mid-1960s through 2025. At the same time, the proportion of the population in older age 
groups has been rising.  
Exhibit 1: Over time the US population has steadily aged  
Proportion of US population by age range (%)

Source: Human Mortality Database; Bank of America Institute calculations

Additionally, there has been a notable acceleration in the relative aging of the population in recent years. For example, between 
1995 and 2025, the proportion of the population aged over 60 rose by almost 10 percentage points (pp) to 25%.  
Several factors have driven this. First, fertility rates in the US, as in much of the world, have fallen sharply. This has slowed 
natural population growth and, by reducing the number of younger people relative to older cohorts, has increased the share of 
the population at older ages. Second, more recently, net inward migration to the US has slowed. Because migrants tend to be 
younger than the overall population, weaker immigration also tends to raise the average age. Finally, rising life expectancy over 
much of the post-war period has allowed more people to survive into older age, further increasing the older population's share.  
Exhibit 2: The trend towards an older composition of the population is expected to continue  
Forecast changes in the share of population by age group relative to 2025 (pp)

Source: United States Census Bureau 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Lower fertility and, more recently, weaker net migration look set to slow overall US population growth in the decades ahead. As a 
result, older Americans are likely to play an increasingly important role in the economy, both as a share of the population and, in 
many age groups, in absolute numbers. The Census Bureau projects the share of the population over 60 will rise another 5pp by 
2055, to stand at nearly 30% of the population (Exhibit 2). For businesses, understanding how the needs and spending patterns 
of older consumers differ from those of younger generations will therefore become increasingly relevant. 
Older age groups allocate their time differently  
Why does this matter? One reason is that older people tend to spend their time and money quite differently from younger adults. 
Most obviously, many work less or have retired altogether. Data from the American Time Use Survey (ATUS) shows that people 
aged 65 and over spend, on average, around two and a half fewer hours per day working than the population aged 15+ (Exhibit 
3). 
That leaves considerably more time for other activities. People aged 65 and over spend around two hours more per day on 
leisure and sports activities, with television viewing accounting for a particularly large share of this additional leisure time 
(Exhibit 4).

Older households also spend differently  
Bank of America internal data on credit and debit card spending provides insights into how these differences in older 
generations’ use of time also impact their spending. Rather than splitting our spending data by generation, as we often do, here 
we split by age bands, allowing for consistent comparisons over time.  
Exhibit 5 shows how the composition of card spending of households aged 61-75 and those older than 75 differs from that of all 
households. A few things stand out. For one, older households devote a greater share of their overall card spending to groceries 
(food and beverages). Households aged 61-75 also allocate more spending to travel (airlines and lodging). And restaurants and 
bars, gasoline, general merchandise and clothing account for a smaller share of older households’ card spending.  
These differences may seem fairly intuitive. Older households have more leisure hours, so spend relatively more on travel to 
enjoy them. Previously we found that more available time may support travel particularly around retirement, although the 
discretionary spending lift can be temporary (for more, read our publication: Paychecks to pensions: The evolution of retiree 
spending).  
Older households are also likely to have less need to update their work wardrobes, so they probably spend less on clothing.

Still, older households’ relatively lower spending on restaurants and bars is a little more curious, perhaps, given dining out could 
be one way of making use of more leisure time. Indeed, when we look at how these spending shares are evolving, we find a 
nuanced picture. While households aged 61-75 do spend relatively less on restaurants and bars than the overall population, they 
appear to be narrowing this gap. But for those older than 75 this is not the case. In our view, the pandemic may still be weighing 
on dining-out spending among this older age group (Exhibit 6).

Aging presents opportunities, but also challenges 
In our view, the aging of the US population presents important opportunities for the economy and businesses. Older consumers 
have more leisure time and businesses that cater to these demands may therefore benefit. 
Moreover, some older consumers hold considerable amounts of net wealth and have been some of the biggest beneficiaries of 
recent rises in equities. Using data from the Federal Reserve, Exhibit 8 shows that as of the second quarter of 2026 the net 
worth of those households aged 55 and over was close to $140 trillion, around three-quarters of the total. And with equity 
markets rising strongly, these age groups have seen a more than 20% increase in their net worth over the past two years, 
potentially enhancing their spending power further.  
But an important caveat is that not all older consumers are in this position. Previous Institute work found evidence consistent 
with equity-market wealth effects being concentrated toward higher-income households (read our publication: Paychecks to 
pensions: The evolution of retiree spending). And Census Bureau research has found that around 14% of Social Security 
recipients aged over 65 rely on it for over 90% of their income. For older households without a wealth cushion, recent elevated 
cost pressures, like gasoline, will likely have put them under more significant financial strain. They will also be more dependent 
on cost-of-living (COLA) increases in Social Security to underpin their spending.  
And, more broadly, outside of card spending, healthcare costs are a much larger factor in older households’ budgets (Exhibit 9). 
The evolution of these costs going forward will be a key variable for older consumers’ budgets.

Methodology  
Selected Bank of America transaction data is used to inform the macroeconomic views expressed in this report and should be considered in the 
context of other economic indicators and publicly available information. In certain instances, the data may provide directional and/or predictive 
value. The data used is not comprehensive; it is based on aggregated and anonymized selections of Bank of America data and may reflect a 
degree of selection bias and limitations on the data available. 
Any payments data represents aggregated spend from US Retail, Preferred, Small Business and Wealth Management clients with a deposit 
account or credit card. Aggregated spend include total credit card, debit card, ACH, wires, bill pay, business/peer-to-peer, cash, and checks.  
Any Small Business payments data represents aggregate spend from Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit 
card. Payroll payments data include channels such as ACH (automated clearing house), bill pay, checks and wire. Bank of America per Small 
Business client data represents activity spending from active Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit card and 
at least one transaction in each month. Small businesses in this report include business clients within Bank of America and generally defined as 
under $5mm in annual sales revenue.  
Unless otherwise stated, data is not adjusted for seasonality, processing days or portfolio changes, and may be subject to periodic revisions.  
The differences between the total and per household card spending growth rate (if discussed) can be explained by the following reasons:  
1. Overall total card spending growth is partially boosted by the growth in the number of active cardholders in our sample. This could be due to 
an increasing customer base or inactive customers using their cards more frequently. 
2. Per household card spending growth only looks at households that complete at least five transactions with Bank of America cards in the 
month. Per household spending growth isolates impacts from a changing sample size, which could be unrelated to underlying economic 
momentum, and potential spending volatility from less active users. 
3. Overall total card spending includes small business card spending while per household card spending does not. 
4. Differences due to using processing dates (total card spending) versus transaction date (per household card spending). 
5. Other differences including household formations due to young adults moving in and out of their parent’s houses during COVID. 
Any household consumer deposit data based on Bank of America internal data is derived by anonymizing and aggregating data from Bank of 
America consumer deposit accounts in the US and analyzing that data at a highly aggregated level. Whenever median household savings and 
checking balances are quoted, the data is based on a fixed cohort of households that had a consumer deposit account (checking and/or savings 
account) for all months from January 2019 through the most current month of data shown.  
Bank of America aggregated credit/debit card spending per household includes spending from active US households only. Only consumer card 
holders making a minimum of five transactions a month are included in the dataset. Spending from corporate cards are excluded. Data 
regarding merchants who receive payments are identified and classified by the Merchant Categorization Code (MCC) defined by financial 
services companies. The data are mapped using proprietary methods from the MCCs to the North American Industry Classification System 
(NAICS), which is also used by the Census Bureau, in order to classify spending data by subsector. Spending data may also be classified by other 
proprietary methods not using MCCs.  
We consider a measure of services necessity spending that includes but is not limited to groceries, utilities, and gasoline. Discretionary services 
includes but is not limited to charitable donations, leisure travel, entertainment, and professional/consumer services. Discretionary retail 
includes but is not limited to general merchandise, miscellaneous, clothing, electronics and furniture. It excludes categories like groceries and 
gasoline. Holiday spending is defined as items in which spending in the November-December period is usually at least 20% of total annual 
spending on the category.  Value and premium grocers are determined judgmentally on a proprietary analysis of merchants by equity analysts.  
Durables spending is defined as spending on electronics, building materials, auto-related spending and furniture. Premium durables spending is 
based on a selection of retailers who are judged to sell relatively higher value products. Conversely, value durables spending is based on a 
selection of retailers who are judged to sell relatively lower value products.  
Lower, middle and higher household income cuts in Bank of America credit and debit card spending per household, and consumer deposit 
account data are based on quantitative estimates of each households’ income. These quantitative estimates are bucketed according to terciles, 
with a third of households placed in each tercile periodically. The lowest tercile represents ‘lower income’, the middle tercile represents ‘middle 
income’ and the highest tercile ‘higher income’. The income thresholds between these terciles will move over time, reflecting any number of 
factors that impact income, including general wage inflation, changes in social security payments and individual households’ income. The 
income and tercile in which a household is categorised are periodically re-assessed.  
Generations, if discussed, are defined as follows: Gen Z, born after 1995; Younger Millennials: born between 1989-1995; Older Millennials: born 
between 1978-1988; Gen Xers: born between 1965-1977; Baby Boomer: 1946-1964; Traditionalists: pre-1946. 
Any reference to card spending per household on gasoline includes all purchases at gasoline stations and might include purchases of non-gas 
items. 
Additional information about the methodology used to aggregate the data is available upon request.

Document 282: GF - PTFE Adoption

Source PDF: all report pdf/GF - PTFE Adoption.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-18

Status: ok

### Page 1

Equity Research 
Sector Report | Overseas Technology｜Oct 15,2026 
PTFE Adoption: 
Reshape the High-End Materials Landscape

Overseas  
Technology

Michelle Jing 
SFC CE No. BUK594 
michellejing@gfgroup.com.hk 
Jeff Pu, CFA 
SFC CE No. BNO719 
jeffpu@gfgroup.com.hk

What's new 
Nvidia showcased its NVLink PTFE board prototype for the first time at OCP Global Summit 2026, highlighting seven supply-chain partners: 
Taiflex (8039 TT), Flexium (6269 TT), TTM Technologies (TTMI US), 3 leading Chinese PCB manufacturers, and 1 leading Chinese CCL 
supplier. We believe this development reinforces our previous view that PTFE remains the most promising material solution for the VR300 
NVL576 switch board, offering the best balance between electrical performance and MP feasibility. 
Comments 
PTFE hybrid remains the preferred solution: As highlighted in our Oct. 5 report, we continue to favor the PTFE hybrid solution for the 
VR300 NVL576 switch board among the various options, given its optimal balance between electrical performance and MP feasibility. 
While pure PTFE remains challenging to process, particularly in lamination and drilling, leading PCB manufacturers have achie ved 
encouraging yields with hybrid structures that combine PTFE signal layers with M9 materials in non-signal layers. 
We like PTFE's long-term adoption potential: PTFE applications in high-frequency and high-speed interconnects could extend beyond 
switch boards. The IEC has published a standard on PTFE-filled CCL, led by the leading Chinese CCL company, highlighting the material's 
relevance to broader circuit-board applications. Our checks also suggest that PTFE materials have entered the testing phase for other 
applications, including compute boards. We therefore remain positive on PTFE's long-term adoption across a broader range of high-end 
PCB applications. 
Limited near-term impact on conventional resin materials, but long-term substitution risks warrant attention: Under the current 
PTFE hybrid design, we expect 8–12 out of 32 layers to adopt PTFE for signal transmission, accounting for less than 50% of the total CCL 
resin content value. Over the longer term, we expect a potential shift toward pure PTFE solutions as improvements in processing 
technology enable commercially viable MP yields, reducing the need for hybrid structures. This could gradually increase substitution risks 
for conventional high-end PCB resin materials, including PPO and HC resin. 
Favor PTFE-exposed suppliers with proven technical and manufacturing capabilities: We remain positive on PTFE-related 
opportunities, particularly suppliers with strong R&D capabilities, proven MP expertise, and established customer qualifications. In CCL, 
we favor Taiflex (8039 TT) and leading Chinese CCL supplier for their PTFE material capabilities and supply-chain exposure. Among the 
five Nvidia-announced PCB suppliers, we favor the leading Chinese PTFE PCB supplier, given its advanced hybrid PTFE processing 
capabilities and encouraging yields. For CCL suppliers that have yet to enter the PTFE supply chain, we recommend monitoring progress 
in developing, while remaining alert to potential market-share losses in their conventional product portfolios. 
Risks  
1) AI demand slowing down; 2) Geo-political risks; 3) Competition.
